===== SOURCE reports/comparison/README.md | SHA256 91d560e73ebf91b5a6c5331c3df6fe23e1e7ef35cb99f9c7d76636ad177c873d =====
# Ten-trial golden-shiner comparison

**The clearest repeated pattern is lower global heading alignment across the 10-, 30- and 70-fish recordings. Circulation is much more variable and strongly dependent on which frames pass the relative-speed cutoff.** The single 151-fish recording does not extend a simple monotone pattern: its median alignment and circulation lie within the ranges of the 70-fish trials.

This extends the same five descriptive chapters to all ten trials: 10,827,539 fish-frame track rows, 266,688,111 directed visual-network rows, and 239,246 observed group frames. These are measurement counts, not independent experimental replicates. There are three trials at each of N=10, 30, 70 and one at N=151. No force, leadership, response-propagation or causal model has been fitted.

## 1. Alignment and circulation: what repeats

![Alignment and circulation across all ten trials](figures/01-alignment-circulation.png)

Each marker is one trial's median. Whiskers show that trial's 5th–95th percentiles over eligible frames, **not confidence intervals**. The statement that group medians separate concerns the markers, not the overlapping whiskers. P is the length of the mean heading vector, from 0 to 1; it is not the percentage of fish aligned. C is coherence of tangential movement around the moving group center after subtracting mean velocity. It is not angular speed or a validated milling classifier.

| Fish | Trial | Median P | P coverage | Median C | C coverage |
|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 0066 | 0.894 | 99.90% | 0.383 | 46.6% |
| 10 | 0105 | 0.919 | 99.80% | 0.287 | 37.3% |
| 10 | 0126 | 0.943 | 99.95% | 0.301 | 72.5% |
| 30 | 0084 | 0.827 | 99.13% | 0.268 | 44.1% |
| 30 | 0115 | 0.809 | 99.80% | 0.344 | 45.7% |
| 30 | 0120 | 0.811 | 99.64% | 0.397 | 55.9% |
| 70 | 0103 | 0.694 | 99.03% | 0.379 | 36.5% |
| 70 | 0107 | 0.509 | 99.29% | 0.781 | 10.6% |
| 70 | 0124 | 0.510 | 99.20% | 0.681 | 5.9% |
| 151 | 150-fish | 0.641 | 98.04% | 0.484 | 87.1% |

Coverage uses all observed group frames as its denominator. P excludes a frame when any fish's heading lies in the temporal support of a flagged large heading jump. C requires valid velocities and at least 80% of fish above 0.5 reference body lengths/s in centroid-relative speed. An undefined C is not zero circulation. The [trial table](tables/trials.csv) also gives velocity-defined frame counts and C coverage among those frames, separating boundary trimming from the relative-motion requirement.

Across the three trials in each smaller group, median P ranges are **0.894–0.943 (N=10), 0.809–0.827 (N=30), and 0.509–0.694 (N=70)**. Every 10-fish median exceeds every 30-fish median, and every 30-fish median exceeds every 70-fish median. This meets the comparison's predeclared, descriptive nonoverlap criterion. It survives raw-heading versus affected-fish-only summaries, exclusion of invalid-length frames, and restriction to the first 17,999 observed frames. P uses raw headings: changing the smoothing window changes P only through the heading-jump exclusion mask, so the window sweep is not an independent test of smoothing the alignment signal. With matched duration, the ranges are 0.882–0.945, 0.794–0.866, and 0.455–0.701.

This describes these recordings; it does not show that adding fish causes weaker social alignment. Global P can fall when different parts of a school move in different directions, including organized rotation. It does not measure local alignment or interaction strength. P also depends on N even for random headings. The dotted approximate-mean reference sqrt(pi/(4N)) is illustrative, not a calibrated null, expected median, significance threshold or correction. The 70-fish trials also have the lowest median speeds, so activity and global alignment co-vary here; speed-stratified alignment has not yet been analyzed. No experimental control or dependent-fish null was fitted.

Median C ranges are **0.287–0.383, 0.268–0.397, and 0.379–0.781** at N=10, 30, 70. Adjacent ranges overlap, so C does not meet the strict monotone criterion. The two high-C 70-fish trials, 0107 and 0124, have only 10.6% and 5.9% primary coverage. Calling them continuously circling schools, or treating those percentages as milling time, would exceed the evidence.

## 2. Why the circulation cutoff matters

![How many fish qualify in the two low-coverage trials](figures/05-circulation-eligibility.png)

This diagnostic counts qualifying fish in each velocity-defined frame. The rule
requires at least 56 of 70 fish; both trials have a median of only 41. Trial 0107
has 2,598 eligible frames out of 24,578 (10.57%); trial 0124 has 1,438 out of 24,533
(5.86%). Only 84 frames in each trial have undefined velocities. Those frames are
outside the histogram but remain in the coverage denominator. The other exclusions
come from the relative-speed/radial-direction rule, not absent track rows. Lowering
only the relative-speed cutoff to 0.25 BL/s raises coverage to 74.6% and 79.1%.
School-relative speed can be small even when fish swim rapidly together. These
percentages measure eligibility under our chosen rule, not time spent circling.

![Circulation eligibility and threshold sensitivities](figures/02-circulation-thresholds.png)

The upper-left panel shows how much data remains; upper-right shows each cutoff's median on its own selected frames. Lower-left holds frames fixed at those qualifying at all three cutoffs, which is exactly the 1.0-eligible, high-relative-motion subset. Fish contributing within those frames can still differ. Lower-right keeps the 0.5-cutoff C calculation fixed and changes its overlapping frame set. Its flat segment from 0.25 to 0.5 is identical by construction, not evidence of robustness. Missing C markers mean undefined summaries, never zero. Eligibility also depends on group size and how fish speeds co-vary under the requirement that at least 80% of fish qualify; coverage is not a group-size-neutral estimate of time spent circling.

Trial 70-fish/0107 illustrates the main selection issue. At 0.25 BL/s, C is measurable in 74.6% of frames and its median is 0.441. At 0.5, coverage falls to 10.6% and the median becomes 0.781. At 1.0 there are no eligible frames; the prespecified three-way intersection is therefore empty. Trial 0124 also has no 1.0-eligible frames.

An explicitly exploratory [pairwise comparison](tables/threshold-pairs.csv) uses the 2,598 frames shared by 0.25 and 0.5 in 0107. On those same frames, the lower-cutoff median is already 0.742, compared with 0.781 at the higher cutoff. Most of the difference from 0.441 to 0.781 therefore reflects selection of different moments; a smaller part reflects which fish contribute within those moments. These are descriptive differences between medians, not causal effects or independent-frame tests. All 30 threshold pairs, including undefined comparisons, are retained with [provenance](tables/threshold-pairs-manifest.json).

The focal fish's contribution to school-average velocity was also checked. Subtracting the other-fish mean rescales relative velocity by N/(N−1), leaving directions unchanged. Applying the equivalent threshold increases C coverage by 0.29–10.45 percentage points across these trials; the largest absolute change in median C is 0.0246. It does not rescue a monotone group-size ordering. This is a cutoff sensitivity, not a correction to the valid moving-centroid description.

## 3. Movement, posture, school size, spacing and visual access

![Other descriptive chapters across trials](figures/04-other-chapters.png)

The table gives the range of the three trial estimates in each smaller group. Movement, spread and spacing use trial medians; visibility uses each trial's mean fraction/degree and its pooled reciprocal-edge fraction.

| Measurement | N=10 | N=30 | N=70 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Median speed, BL/s |1.376–2.259|1.378–1.593|0.713–1.160|
| Median RMS radius, BL |2.202–2.970|3.707–5.069|4.979–5.751|
| Median nearest-neighbor spacing, BL |1.114–1.467|0.968–1.274|0.876–1.063|
| Mean visible neighbors |6.555–6.929|14.777–16.979|21.565–25.321|
| Mean fraction of other fish visible |72.83–76.99%|50.95–58.55%|31.25–36.70%|
| Reciprocal-edge fraction |82.73–85.08%|76.17–77.57%|69.83–72.25%|

**Movement:** all three 70-fish trials have lower median swimming speed than every 10- or 30-fish trial. This survives matching duration. The 10- and 30-fish ranges overlap, so there is no strict three-group monotone speed ordering. Trial 0084 is duration-sensitive: median speed changes from 1.378 to 1.703 BL/s in the first 17,999 frames, an increase of 23.6%. Recorded speed differences should not be treated as context-free properties of group size.

**Posture:** signed curvature and turn-rate distributions include both signs and overlap between groups. Recorded signed-curvature medians are positive in all ten trials (0.015–0.123); this could reflect biological asymmetry or pose-reconstruction bias. The sign also follows the chosen image-coordinate and head-to-tail conventions; it is not by itself evidence of an anatomical left-right preference. Signed summaries can hide cancellation and do not measure turning or bending intensity. The curvature estimate comes from a local cubic fit around the midpoint of the recorded midline; it is not a direct force measurement. No robust monotone claim about bending intensity is made.

**School organization and spatial relationships:** school-spread medians separate between N=10 and 30, but the N=30 and 70 ranges overlap at full duration and separate only in the matched prefix. Some footprint growth is geometrically expected when more fish occupy a group at comparable local spacing. Typical nearest-neighbor distances remain around one reference body length. The group center is the arithmetic mean of smoothed fish positions; RMS radius is the square root of mean squared distance from that center. A strict three-group spread ordering is therefore not robust to the time window. Nearest spacing overlaps between adjacent group sizes. A larger total school footprint does not by itself establish wider local spacing.

**Visual access:** the number of geometrically visible neighbors rises across 10→30→70, while the fraction of the group visible and reciprocal-edge fraction fall. The adjacent ranges do not overlap, and these orderings survive matched duration and removal of invalid-length frames. These are properties of the supplied ray-cast geometry; occlusion and the growing number of potential neighbors can themselves produce size-dependent degree and visible fraction. They do not establish attention, information use, response strength or influence. An observed edge means observer id1 sees id2; reciprocity is the fraction of directed edges with a reverse edge, pooled over covered frames.

## 4. The 151-fish recording is a separate comparison

Its median P is 0.641 and C is 0.484, with C defined in 87.1% of observed frames. Both medians lie within the 70-fish ranges. Its median speed is 1.861 BL/s, above the 70-fish range, so the lower speed observed at 70 does not continue in this recording.

It contains 17,999 frames in one segment with no internal missing frames, versus seven segments and six missing join frames in each smaller trial. It uses a different camera, and the source reports visible bodies about 15% longer than the stored reference length. With the illustrative L×1.15 scenario, speed becomes 1.618 BL/s, RMS radius changes from 8.927 to 7.763 BL, and nearest spacing from 0.985 to 0.857 BL. Reapplying the circulation cutoff reduces coverage from 87.1% to 77.4% and changes median C from 0.484 to 0.507. P is unchanged by this length rescaling.

This scenario is not a calibrated correction: it cannot repair anatomical midline differences or regenerate the visual network from corrected outlines. **The 151-fish curvature and visibility measurements are excluded from group-size claims.** One recording under different conditions cannot establish a group-size effect. Source timing is 29.97 fps and time=frame/29.97; we have not independently verified this video's timing or camera orientation. Signed circulation is not compared across cameras.

## 5. Quality checks, time series and what remains uncertain

![Alignment and circulation time series for all ten trials](figures/03-collective-timelines.png)

Time series retain every observed group frame and break at segment boundaries, gaps and undefined measurements. They show variation during each recording; no persistence scale, transition rate or state-duration estimate is claimed.

The measured input columns passed finite-value, complete-fish-set, unique-key and within-block timing checks. Every network row was processed and checked, including duplicate edges, endpoints, ray/angular consistency and distances. Every observed track frame had a represented network, so covered-frame degree summaries include zero-degree fish. Entirely missing edge-list frames would remain unknown. This audits the selected measurement columns and supplied geometry; it does not validate unused pose columns or prove that tracking identities and headings are biologically correct.

There are 22 nonpositive-length fish observations across 14 trial-frames, with collapsed posture geometry. Reference L uses positive lengths only and curvature is undefined on those rows. Primary finite positions/headings and the supplied network remain recorded measurements. A separate sensitivity removes affected frames, recomputes motion with gap guards, and excludes those frames from network summaries. The largest changes in median P and C are only 0.000110 and 0.0000731, respectively. This demonstrates robustness of these summaries to those particular flagged frames, not that all tracking errors have been eliminated.

Primary P coverage is 98.0–99.95%. Dropping only affected fish instead of entire heading-flagged frames changes median P by at most 0.00144 and preserves the 10→30→70 ordering. Added-frame P summaries and all changing denominators are retained in the compact tables. Heading anomalies remain candidates for video inspection; they are not established head-tail flips. Suggested source-frame windows include 0084: 17057–17066; 0107: 9949–9958 and 19468–19478; 0103: 16807–16831; 0124: 18690–18716. Simultaneous anomalies could be tracking defects or real rapid collective turns, which must be checked against video. No additional videos were inspected for this comparison; the original pilot retains its earlier selected overlays.

**Computational QA:** 131 tests and 8 subtests pass. The [validator](validation.json) verifies all recorded input/source/output hashes and identical analysis source/config across the ten runs. All ten primary quantile dictionaries and network summaries for 0066 exactly reproduce the pilot. The largest absolute difference among fifty independently recalculated sampled P values is 2.220446049250313×10⁻¹⁶ (approximately 2.22×10⁻¹⁶). This is coordinator-run QA, not an independent rerun of every metric.

**Review status:** Claude Opus 5.5 independently passed the [implementation snapshot](../../docs/workflow/reviews/comparison-implementation/review.md); its [notes were dispositioned](../../docs/workflow/reviews/comparison-implementation/adjudication.md). The [numerical-results review](../../docs/workflow/reviews/comparison-results/review.md) found no contradiction or rerun-level defect and requested narrative corrections. The corrected report received [PASS in focused confirmation](../../docs/workflow/reviews/comparison-results-fixes/review.md), accepted for local sharing with the stated caveats; see the [final disposition](../../docs/workflow/reviews/comparison-results-fixes/adjudication.md). Claude receives explicit source/results text without tools: it does not execute the data pipeline or inspect rendered images. The coordinator inspected the plots. Independent computational reproduction remains a separate unfinished gate.

## A defensible Monday summary

“We extended the descriptive analysis to all ten recordings. Across the three trials at each of 10, 30 and 70 fish, larger groups had lower whole-school heading alignment; that ordering survived our heading-filter, matched-duration and flagged-geometry checks. Circulation varied substantially between trials and depended strongly on the relative-speed cutoff, so we cannot yet label milling states or infer how long they lasted. Typical local spacing stayed around one body length, while geometric visual access covered a smaller fraction of larger groups. The single 151-fish recording’s alignment fell within the 70-fish range, so it does not extend the ordering; it also has a different camera. These results identify behavior to investigate; they do not yet identify social forces, leadership or causation.”

## Files and reproduction

[Trial estimates and denominators](tables/trials.csv) · [Threshold summaries](tables/thresholds.csv) · [All sensitivity values](tables/sensitivity.csv) · [Metric quantiles](tables/metrics.csv) · [Plot inputs](plot-manifest.json) · [Run log](run-log.txt) · [Download verification](download-log.txt).

Per-trial summaries, configs, histogram tables and provenance are in `trials/<trial>/`. Large frame-level CSVs remain local ignored caches; scripts regenerate them. Raw tracks/networks stay in ignored `data/trials/`, outside Git. PNG figures have matching editable-text SVGs. Use the [repository reproduction commands](../../README.md#compare-all-ten-trials) with a new output directory. Measurement definitions and predeclared comparison criteria are in [comparison methods](../../docs/comparison-methods.md) and [pilot methods](../../docs/methods.md).

The next scientific work is to inspect selected high-C intervals against video, compare local versus global alignment, and then test predictive response models with explicit nulls and a validation protocol that accounts for exposure to all ten trials. Independent computational reproduction is still required before treating this as a fully independently verified release. Marco authorized publication of this descriptive package on November 7, 2026; the [session account](../../docs/session-summary.md) and [workflow status](../../docs/workflow/status.md) record its scope and review evidence.

# Validation assessment

**Share with noted caveats.** All ten trials are analyzed and the descriptive comparison has passed coordinator computational QA and Claude's independent static review after narrative corrections.

## Evidence

- [Machine-readable QA](validation.json): all recorded hashes match; source/configuration agree across all ten runs; metadata and network histograms reconcile. Python/package versions also match across runs.
- Exact pilot parity: ten primary quantile dictionaries, visual-degree quantiles, mean degree and reciprocity match the retained 0066 pilot.
- Fifty sampled alignment values independently recalculated from raw headings agree within floating-point precision; maximum absolute difference 2.220446049250313e-16.
- [Test evidence](../test-results-comparison.txt): 131 tests and 8 subtests passed. No numerical source changed after this run.
- All four final figure sets visually inspected: clear units, trial-level markers, within-trial percentile ranges rather than confidence intervals, and explicit eligibility/151-fish caveats. Local report links checked.
- [Claude implementation review](../../docs/workflow/reviews/comparison-implementation/review.md), [results review](../../docs/workflow/reviews/comparison-results/review.md), and [final PASS](../../docs/workflow/reviews/comparison-results-fixes/review.md) are retained with exact supplied snapshots and coordinator dispositions.

## Required caveats

Trials are the descriptive comparison units; fish-frames are temporally and socially dependent. Raw P and C have finite-group behavior and no calibrated experimental null. Circulation summaries are conditional on eligibility, with particularly sparse coverage in 70-fish/0107 and 0124. The 151-fish recording is a single different-camera trial and does not continue the P ordering; its curvature and visibility are excluded from group-size claims. Rare collapsed-posture observations are disclosed and sensitivity-tested. Additional videos were not inspected, and finite tracked values do not prove biological correctness.

Claude did not execute code or inspect figures. Independent computational reproduction remains pending. This assessment supports the local descriptive report, not a causal model, behavioral-state classifier or independently reproduced scientific release.

===== SOURCE scripts/plot_eligibility.py | SHA256 78bed0167adb2f6be19b6ab35aff8108aa6cce52cc7544eb83ab5e589432d46b =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Explain circulation coverage from frozen per-frame comparison outputs.

Run after the comparison pipeline has created collective.csv for both trials.
Only this script's derived table, manifest, and figure are written.
"""
import argparse
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path

import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


TRIALS = ("70-fish__0107", "70-fish__0124")
REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
COUNT_ATOL = 1e-8


def digest(path):
    return hashlib.sha256(Path(path).read_bytes()).hexdigest()


def eligibility_summary(stem, summary, config, collective):
    """Recover integer fish counts and cross-check the actual reporting mask."""
    fish = summary["fish"]
    if isinstance(fish, bool) or not isinstance(fish, int) or fish < 2:
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: fish must be an integer >= 2")
    fraction = float(config["circulation_min_fraction"])
    speed = float(config["low_speed_bl_s"])
    if not np.isfinite(fraction) or not 0 < fraction <= 1:
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: invalid minimum fraction")
    if not np.isfinite(speed) or speed <= 0:
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: invalid speed cutoff")
    # Use the same >= fraction comparison as the original metric. This avoids
    # a floating-point ceil error at exact integer-count boundaries.
    required = int(np.flatnonzero(np.arange(fish + 1) / fish >= fraction)[0])
    required_columns = {"frame", "eligible_fraction", "circulation"}
    if not required_columns.issubset(collective.columns):
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: missing required columns")
    frames = collective["frame"].to_numpy(dtype=float)
    if (not len(frames) or not np.isfinite(frames).all()
            or not np.equal(frames, np.rint(frames)).all()
            or not (np.diff(frames) > 0).all()):
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: frames must be unique increasing integers")
    if len(frames) != summary["quality"]["frames"]:
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: tracked-frame denominator mismatch")
    fractions = collective["eligible_fraction"].to_numpy(dtype=float)
    circulation = collective["circulation"].to_numpy(dtype=float)
    if np.isinf(fractions).any() or np.isinf(circulation).any():
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: infinities are not missing values")
    defined = np.isfinite(fractions)
    if ((fractions[defined] < 0) | (fractions[defined] > 1)).any():
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: eligible fractions outside [0, 1]")
    scaled_counts = fractions[defined] * fish
    counts = np.rint(scaled_counts).astype(int)
    if not np.allclose(scaled_counts, counts, rtol=0, atol=COUNT_ATOL):
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: eligible fractions do not encode integer fish counts")
    if "eligible_fish_count" in collective:
        saved = collective["eligible_fish_count"].to_numpy(dtype=float)
        if (np.isinf(saved).any() or not np.array_equal(np.isfinite(saved), defined)
                or not np.allclose(saved[defined], counts, rtol=0, atol=COUNT_ATOL)):
            raise ValueError(f"{stem}: saved eligible fish count mismatch")
    accepted_mask = np.zeros(len(frames), dtype=bool)
    accepted_mask[defined] = counts >= required
    finite_c = np.isfinite(circulation)
    if not np.array_equal(accepted_mask, finite_c):
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: qualifying frame mask differs from finite circulation")
    if ((circulation[finite_c] < -1e-12) | (circulation[finite_c] > 1 + 1e-12)).any():
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: circulation outside [0, 1]")
    accepted = int(accepted_mask.sum())
    coverage = accepted / len(frames)
    if accepted != summary["circulation"]["n"]:
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: circulation summary count mismatch")
    if not np.isclose(coverage, summary["circulation_eligible_frame_fraction"], rtol=0, atol=1e-12):
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: circulation summary coverage mismatch")
    # The saved eligibility mask cannot separate radius failures from relative
    # speed failures. It can verify that undefined frames have undefined velocity.
    velocity_undefined = None
    if "centroid_speed_bl_s" in collective:
        centroid_speed = collective["centroid_speed_bl_s"].to_numpy(dtype=float)
        if np.isinf(centroid_speed).any():
            raise ValueError(f"{stem}: infinite centroid speed")
        velocity_undefined = int((~np.isfinite(centroid_speed)).sum())
        if not np.array_equal(~np.isfinite(centroid_speed), ~defined):
            raise ValueError(f"{stem}: undefined eligibility and velocity masks differ")
    return {
        "trial": stem,
        "fish": fish,
        "low_speed_bl_s": speed,
        "circulation_min_fraction": fraction,
        "required_qualifying_fish": required,
        "tracked_frames": len(frames),
        "eligibility_defined_frames": int(defined.sum()),
        "eligibility_undefined_frames": int((~defined).sum()),
        "velocity_undefined_frames": velocity_undefined,
        "accepted_frames": accepted,
        "defined_but_below_requirement_frames": int(defined.sum()) - accepted,
        "coverage_all_tracked_frames": coverage,
        "median_qualifying_fish": float(np.median(counts)) if len(counts) else None,
        "qualifying_fish_p05_p95": np.quantile(counts, [.05, .95]).tolist() if len(counts) else None,
        "histogram_fish_count": list(range(fish + 1)),
        "histogram_frame_count": np.bincount(counts, minlength=fish + 1).tolist(),
    }


def load_trial(root, stem):
    folder = root / "trials" / stem
    paths = {name: folder / name for name in ("summary.json", "config.json", "collective.csv", "provenance.json")}
    provenance = json.loads(paths["provenance.json"].read_text())
    for name in ("summary.json", "config.json", "collective.csv"):
        if digest(paths[name]) != provenance["output_hashes"].get(name):
            raise ValueError(f"{stem}: saved provenance hash mismatch for {name}")
    metric = "src/shiners/metrics.py"
    if digest(REPO / metric) != provenance["source_hashes"].get(metric):
        raise ValueError(f"{stem}: metric source differs from the frozen run")
    result = eligibility_summary(
        stem, json.loads(paths["summary.json"].read_text()),
        json.loads(paths["config.json"].read_text()), pd.read_csv(paths["collective.csv"]),
    )
    hashes = {str(path.relative_to(root)): digest(path) for path in paths.values()}
    return result, hashes


def draw(rows, figure_dir):
    settings = {(r["fish"], r["required_qualifying_fish"], r["low_speed_bl_s"], r["circulation_min_fraction"]) for r in rows}
    if len(settings) != 1:
        raise ValueError("The shared comparison figure requires matching fish counts and cutoffs")
    fish, required, speed, fraction = settings.pop()
    plt.rcParams["svg.fonttype"] = "none"
    plt.rcParams["svg.hashsalt"] = "shiners-circulation-eligibility"
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, len(rows), figsize=(12, 5.6), sharey=True, squeeze=False)
    maximum = max(max(row["histogram_frame_count"]) for row in rows)
    for ax, row in zip(axes[0], rows):
        x = np.asarray(row["histogram_fish_count"])
        ax.bar(x, row["histogram_frame_count"], width=.96,
               color=np.where(x >= required, "#d77b16", "#7892a4"))
        ax.axvline(required - .5, color="black", linestyle="--", linewidth=1.3)
        ax.set_xlim(-.5, fish + .5)
        ax.set_ylim(0, max(1, maximum) * 1.12)
        ax.set_xticks(sorted(set(range(0, fish + 1, max(1, fish // 7))) | {required, fish}))
        ax.set_xlabel("Number of fish qualifying in that frame")
        ax.set_title(f"Trial {row['trial'].split('__')[1]}: {row['accepted_frames']:,} / {row['tracked_frames']:,} frames qualify\n"
                     f"({row['coverage_all_tracked_frames']:.1%} of all tracked frames)", fontsize=11)
        median = row["median_qualifying_fish"]
        median_label = f"{median:g} of {fish}" if median is not None else "unavailable"
        ax.text(.025, .95, f"Median qualifying count: {median_label}", transform=ax.transAxes, va="top", fontsize=10)
        ax.text(required + .6, .66 * max(1, maximum), f"Requirement: {required} fish", rotation=90, fontsize=10, va="center")
        ax.spines[["top", "right"]].set_visible(False)
        ax.set_axisbelow(True)
        ax.grid(axis="y", alpha=.15)
    axes[0, 0].set_ylabel("Number of frames")
    fig.suptitle(f"Circulation is reported only when at least {required} of {fish} fish qualify (≥{fraction:.0%})", fontsize=14, y=.98)
    unknown = "; ".join(f"{r['trial'].split('__')[1]}: {r['eligibility_undefined_frames']:,}" for r in rows)
    undefined_kind = "velocity-undefined" if all(r["velocity_undefined_frames"] is not None for r in rows) else "eligibility-undefined"
    fig.text(.07, .025,
             "Blue bars: too few fish qualify. Orange bars: the frame passes the reporting rule.\n"
             f"Qualifying fish need speed ≥{speed:g} body lengths/s relative to the moving school, plus the tiny radius guard (r > 10⁻⁶ body lengths).\n"
             f"The {undefined_kind} frames ({unknown}) are absent from the bars, but included in each coverage denominator.\n"
             "Coverage measures where circulation is defined under this rule; it is not the fraction of time the school circles.",
             fontsize=9, va="bottom")
    fig.subplots_adjust(left=.07, right=.99, bottom=.27, top=.81, wspace=.08)
    figure_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    paths = []
    for suffix in ("png", "svg"):
        path = figure_dir / f"05-circulation-eligibility.{suffix}"
        metadata = {"Date": None} if suffix == "svg" else None
        fig.savefig(path, dpi=180, bbox_inches="tight", metadata=metadata)
        paths.append(path)
    plt.close(fig)
    return paths


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument("--root", type=Path, default=Path("reports/comparison"), help="comparison output root")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    rows, input_hashes = [], {}
    for stem in TRIALS:
        row, hashes = load_trial(args.root, stem)
        rows.append(row)
        input_hashes.update(hashes)
    outputs = draw(rows, args.root / "figures")
    table = args.root / "tables" / "circulation-eligibility.json"
    table.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    payload = {
        "scope": "Descriptive counts of tracked frames, not independent samples or a milling-time estimate.",
        "eligibility": "Saved eligible_fraction includes school-relative speed >= the configured cutoff and radius > 1e-6 body lengths. These components are not separately audited here.",
        "count_reconstruction_absolute_tolerance": COUNT_ATOL,
        "trials": rows,
    }
    table.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2, allow_nan=False) + "\n")
    outputs.append(table)
    manifest = {
        "reproduce": "python scripts/plot_eligibility.py --root reports/comparison",
        "input_path_base": "comparison root supplied by --root",
        "source_path_base": "repository root",
        "source_hashes": {p: digest(REPO / p) for p in ("scripts/plot_eligibility.py", "src/shiners/metrics.py")},
        "input_hashes": input_hashes,
        "output_hashes": {str(p.relative_to(args.root)): digest(p) for p in outputs},
        "packages": {"numpy": np.__version__, "pandas": pd.__version__, "matplotlib": matplotlib.__version__},
        "validation": ["inputs match frozen trial provenance", "metric source matches frozen trial provenance", "integer qualifying counts", "exact finite-circulation frame-mask match", "summary counts and all-tracked-frame coverage match"],
    }
    (table.parent / "circulation-eligibility-manifest.json").write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2) + "\n")
    print(json.dumps({r["trial"]: {k: r[k] for k in ("accepted_frames", "tracked_frames", "eligibility_undefined_frames", "required_qualifying_fish", "median_qualifying_fish")} for r in rows}, indent=2))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

===== SOURCE tests/test_plot_eligibility.py | SHA256 f537132f4b43c193140ec6f2fd2faa1e44a554ebb165ffdc2159665f30ab2ddf =====
"""Check coverage accounting, integer boundaries, and corrupt saved inputs."""
import importlib.util
import json
from pathlib import Path
import tempfile
import unittest

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("plot_eligibility", Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "scripts/plot_eligibility.py")
plot = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
spec.loader.exec_module(plot)


def fixture(fish=70, counts=(np.nan, 0, 41, 55, 56, 70)):
    counts = np.asarray(counts, dtype=float)
    fractions = counts / fish
    valid = np.isfinite(counts) & (fractions >= .8)
    summary = {"fish": fish, "quality": {"frames": len(counts)},
               "circulation": {"n": int(valid.sum())},
               "circulation_eligible_frame_fraction": float(valid.mean())}
    config = {"circulation_min_fraction": .8, "low_speed_bl_s": .5}
    frame = pd.DataFrame({"frame": np.arange(1, len(counts) + 1),
                          "eligible_fraction": fractions,
                          "eligible_fish_count": counts,
                          "circulation": np.where(valid, .7, np.nan),
                          "centroid_speed_bl_s": np.where(np.isfinite(counts), .1, np.nan)})
    return summary, config, frame


class EligibilityTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_mixed_frames_keep_unavailable_in_coverage_but_not_histogram(self):
        result = plot.eligibility_summary("test", *fixture())
        self.assertEqual(result["required_qualifying_fish"], 56)
        self.assertEqual(result["accepted_frames"], 2)
        self.assertEqual(result["defined_but_below_requirement_frames"], 3)
        self.assertEqual(result["velocity_undefined_frames"], 1)
        self.assertEqual(result["coverage_all_tracked_frames"], 2 / 6)
        self.assertEqual(sum(result["histogram_frame_count"]), 5)
        self.assertEqual(result["histogram_frame_count"][0], 1)
        self.assertEqual(result["median_qualifying_fish"], 55)

    def test_fractional_requirement_rounds_up_and_tolerates_csv_roundoff(self):
        s, c, f = fixture(151, (np.nan, 120, 121, 151))
        f.loc[2, "eligible_fraction"] += 1e-12
        result = plot.eligibility_summary("test", s, c, f)
        self.assertEqual(result["required_qualifying_fish"], 121)
        self.assertEqual(result["accepted_frames"], 2)
        self.assertEqual(result["low_speed_bl_s"], .5)

    def test_all_unavailable_has_no_histogram_or_median(self):
        result = plot.eligibility_summary("test", *fixture(10, (np.nan, np.nan)))
        self.assertEqual(result["coverage_all_tracked_frames"], 0)
        self.assertIsNone(result["median_qualifying_fish"])
        self.assertEqual(sum(result["histogram_frame_count"]), 0)

    def test_count_match_alone_does_not_hide_wrong_accepted_frames(self):
        s, c, f = fixture()
        f.loc[3, "circulation"], f.loc[4, "circulation"] = .7, np.nan
        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError, "frame mask differs"):
            plot.eligibility_summary("test", s, c, f)

    def test_loader_rejects_changed_input_against_frozen_provenance(self):
        s, c, f = fixture()
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as directory:
            root = Path(directory)
            folder = root / "trials" / "test"
            folder.mkdir(parents=True)
            (folder / "summary.json").write_text(json.dumps(s))
            (folder / "config.json").write_text(json.dumps(c))
            f.to_csv(folder / "collective.csv", index=False)
            provenance = {
                "output_hashes": {name: plot.digest(folder / name) for name in ("summary.json", "config.json", "collective.csv")},
                "source_hashes": {"src/shiners/metrics.py": plot.digest(plot.REPO / "src/shiners/metrics.py")},
            }
            (folder / "provenance.json").write_text(json.dumps(provenance))
            row, hashes = plot.load_trial(root, "test")
            self.assertEqual(row["accepted_frames"], 2)
            self.assertIn("trials/test/provenance.json", hashes)
            # The numerical values are unchanged, but source bytes no longer
            # match the frozen run. The loader must still reject the artifact.
            with (folder / "collective.csv").open("a") as stream:
                stream.write("\n")
            with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError, "provenance hash mismatch"):
                plot.load_trial(root, "test")

    def test_rejects_bad_fractions_counts_and_denominators(self):
        mutations = [
            (lambda s, c, f: f.__setitem__("eligible_fraction", [np.nan, 0, .501, 55/70, .8, 1]), "integer fish"),
            (lambda s, c, f: f.loc.__setitem__((1, "eligible_fraction"), np.inf), "infinities"),
            (lambda s, c, f: f.loc.__setitem__((1, "eligible_fraction"), -.1), "outside"),
            (lambda s, c, f: f.loc.__setitem__((4, "eligible_fish_count"), 55), "saved eligible"),
            (lambda s, c, f: f.loc.__setitem__((1, "frame"), 1), "unique increasing"),
            (lambda s, c, f: s["quality"].update(frames=7), "denominator"),
            (lambda s, c, f: s.update(circulation_eligible_frame_fraction=.4), "coverage"),
            (lambda s, c, f: c.update(circulation_min_fraction=1.1), "minimum fraction"),
            (lambda s, c, f: f.loc.__setitem__((1, "centroid_speed_bl_s"), np.nan), "velocity masks"),
        ]
        for mutate, message in mutations:
            with self.subTest(message=message):
                s, c, f = fixture()
                mutate(s, c, f)
                with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError, message):
                    plot.eligibility_summary("test", s, c, f)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

===== SOURCE src/shiners/metrics.py | SHA256 72b20c3d6815a6ae607fdce8af61a9c8caa68e2dc2e5e00688c5689fb62a3cc6 =====
"""Descriptive pilot measurements defined in docs/methods.md.

Inputs are one trial. Pixels use image coordinates (y down). Undefined quantities
remain NaN; functions do not impute missing samples or infer behavioral responses.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from scipy.signal import savgol_filter


def _single_trial(tracks):
    for column in ("group", "sequence"):
        if column in tracks and tracks[column].nunique(dropna=False) > 1:
            raise ValueError("Metrics require one trial at a time")
    if tracks.empty:
        raise ValueError("Empty tracks")
    if tracks.duplicated(["frame", "id"]).any():
        raise ValueError("Duplicate track keys")


def _scale(length_scale):
    if not np.isfinite(length_scale) or length_scale <= 0:
        raise ValueError("Length scale must be finite and positive")


def _dense(tracks, columns):
    """A frame-by-fish view with explicit identical fish coverage."""
    _single_trial(tracks)
    frames = np.sort(tracks.frame.unique())
    fish = np.sort(tracks.id.unique())
    ordered = tracks.sort_values(["frame", "id"])
    if len(ordered) != len(frames) * len(fish):
        raise ValueError("Incomplete fish set")
    # Unique keys plus the full Cartesian cardinality ensures every fish/frame.
    arrays = {column: ordered[column].to_numpy(dtype=float).reshape(len(frames), len(fish))
              for column in columns}
    return frames, fish, ordered, arrays


def kinematics(tracks, window=11, order=3):
    """Return original rows/index plus segment- and gap-safe temporal derivatives.

    vx/vy: pixels/s; ax/ay: pixels/s²; turn_rate: radians/s (clockwise positive).
    Discard half a smoothing window plus one sample at both block ends, so the
    known noisier first/last segment samples do not enter retained estimates.
    """
    _single_trial(tracks)
    if not isinstance(window, (int, np.integer)) or window < 3 or window % 2 != 1:
        raise ValueError("Window must be an odd integer >= 3")
    if not isinstance(order, (int, np.integer)) or order < 2 or order >= window:
        raise ValueError("Polynomial order must be >= 2 and below window")
    if not np.isfinite(tracks[["time", "frame", "X", "Y", "ANGLE"]].to_numpy(dtype=float)).all():
        raise ValueError("Nonfinite kinematics input")
    result = tracks.copy()
    output = {name: np.full(len(tracks), np.nan) for name in ["xs", "ys", "vx", "vy", "ax", "ay", "turn_rate"]}
    jump = np.zeros(len(tracks), dtype=bool)
    heading_valid = np.ones(len(tracks), dtype=bool)
    half = window // 2
    trim = half + 1
    # groupby.indices uses positional indexes, preserving even nonunique row labels.
    for positions in tracks.groupby(["id", "segment"], sort=False).indices.values():
        positions = positions[np.argsort(tracks.frame.to_numpy()[positions], kind="stable")]
        frame = tracks.frame.to_numpy()[positions]
        for block in np.split(positions, np.flatnonzero(np.diff(frame) != 1) + 1):
            if len(block) < 2:
                continue
            sample = tracks.iloc[block]
            heading = sample.ANGLE.to_numpy(dtype=float)
            step = (np.diff(heading) + np.pi) % (2*np.pi) - np.pi
            flagged = np.flatnonzero(np.abs(step) > np.pi/2)
            block_jump = np.zeros(len(block), dtype=bool)
            block_jump[flagged] = True
            block_jump[flagged+1] = True
            jump[block] = block_jump
            contaminated = np.convolve(np.pad(block_jump, (half, half)), np.ones(window), mode="valid") > 0
            heading_valid[block] = ~contaminated
            delta = np.diff(sample.time.to_numpy(dtype=float))
            dt = float(np.median(delta))
            if dt <= 0 or not np.allclose(delta, dt, rtol=1e-6, atol=1e-10):
                raise ValueError("Irregular or nonpositive time increments within a continuous block")
            if len(block) <= 2*trim:
                continue
            interior = block[trim:-trim]
            for coordinate, velocity, acceleration in [("X", "vx", "ax"), ("Y", "vy", "ay")]:
                values = sample[coordinate].to_numpy(dtype=float)
                smooth = savgol_filter(values, window, order, mode="interp")
                output["xs" if coordinate == "X" else "ys"][interior] = smooth[trim:-trim]
                for derivative, name in [(1, velocity), (2, acceleration)]:
                    fitted = savgol_filter(values, window, order, deriv=derivative, delta=dt, mode="interp")
                    output[name][interior] = fitted[trim:-trim]
            turn = savgol_filter(np.unwrap(heading), window, order, deriv=1, delta=dt, mode="interp")
            output["turn_rate"][interior] = turn[trim:-trim]
    output["turn_rate"][~heading_valid] = np.nan
    for name, values in output.items():
        result[name] = values
    result["heading_jump"] = jump
    result["heading_valid"] = heading_valid
    axis_error = np.full(len(tracks), np.nan)
    if all(column in tracks for column in ["poseX0", "poseY0", "poseX20", "poseY20"]):
        dx = (tracks.poseX0-tracks.poseX20).to_numpy(dtype=float)
        dy = (tracks.poseY0-tracks.poseY20).to_numpy(dtype=float)
        valid_axis = np.isfinite(dx) & np.isfinite(dy) & (np.hypot(dx, dy) > 1e-8)
        axis_error[valid_axis] = ((tracks.ANGLE.to_numpy()[valid_axis] - np.arctan2(dy[valid_axis], dx[valid_axis]) + np.pi)
                                  % (2*np.pi) - np.pi)
    result["heading_axis_error"] = axis_error
    return result


def curvature(tracks, neighborhood=7):
    """Signed midpoint curvature in 1/pixel from a local cubic midline fit.

    Equal arc-spacing coordinates are centered on keypoint 10. Points 21 and 22
    (eyes) are never used. Positive sign is clockwise head-to-tail bending.
    """
    if (not isinstance(neighborhood, (int, np.integer)) or neighborhood < 5
            or neighborhood > 21 or neighborhood % 2 != 1):
        raise ValueError("Curvature neighborhood must be odd, between 5 and 21")
    indices = np.arange(10-neighborhood//2, 11+neighborhood//2)
    u = indices / 20.0 - 0.5
    design = np.column_stack([np.ones(neighborhood), u, u*u, u*u*u])
    inverse = np.linalg.pinv(design)
    x = tracks[[f"poseX{i}" for i in indices]].to_numpy(dtype=float)
    y = tracks[[f"poseY{i}" for i in indices]].to_numpy(dtype=float)
    dx, dy = x @ inverse[1], y @ inverse[1]
    ddx, ddy = 2 * (x @ inverse[2]), 2 * (y @ inverse[2])
    speed = np.hypot(dx, dy)
    valid = np.isfinite(x).all(axis=1) & np.isfinite(y).all(axis=1) & (speed > 1e-8)
    out = np.full(len(tracks), np.nan)
    out[valid] = (dx[valid]*ddy[valid] - dy[valid]*ddx[valid]) / speed[valid]**3
    return out


def collective(tracks_with_kinematics, length_scale, low_speed=.5, min_fraction=.8):
    """Per-frame heading alignment, spread and centroid-relative circulation."""
    _scale(length_scale)
    if not np.isfinite(low_speed) or low_speed <= 0 or not 0 < min_fraction <= 1:
        raise ValueError("Low speed must be positive and min_fraction in (0,1]")
    x_col, y_col = ("xs", "ys") if {"xs", "ys"}.issubset(tracks_with_kinematics.columns) else ("X", "Y")
    frames, fish, ordered, a = _dense(tracks_with_kinematics, [x_col, y_col, "ANGLE", "vx", "vy"])
    position = np.stack([a[x_col], a[y_col]], axis=2)
    velocity = np.stack([a["vx"], a["vy"]], axis=2)
    if not np.isfinite(a["ANGLE"]).all():
        raise ValueError("Nonfinite collective heading")
    relative = position - position.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)
    radius = np.linalg.norm(relative, axis=2)
    centroid_v = velocity.mean(axis=1)
    all_velocity = np.isfinite(velocity).all(axis=(1, 2))
    all_geometry = np.isfinite(position).all(axis=(1, 2))
    all_valid = all_velocity & all_geometry
    relative_v = velocity - centroid_v[:, None, :]
    relative_speed = np.linalg.norm(relative_v, axis=2)
    eligible = ((radius > 1e-6*length_scale) & (relative_speed >= low_speed*length_scale)
                & all_valid[:, None])
    fractions = eligible.mean(axis=1)
    # Eligibility is unknown when the centroid-relative velocities cannot be computed.
    fractions = np.where(all_valid, fractions, np.nan)
    cross = relative[:, :, 0]*relative_v[:, :, 1] - relative[:, :, 1]*relative_v[:, :, 0]
    normalized = np.zeros_like(cross)
    np.divide(cross, radius*relative_speed, out=normalized, where=eligible)
    counts = eligible.sum(axis=1)
    circulation = np.full(len(frames), np.nan)
    signed = np.full(len(frames), np.nan)
    reference = np.full(len(frames), np.nan)
    valid = all_valid & (fractions >= min_fraction) & (counts > 0)
    signed[valid] = np.clip(normalized[valid].sum(axis=1)/counts[valid], -1, 1)
    circulation[valid] = np.abs(signed[valid])
    reference[valid] = np.sqrt(1/(np.pi*counts[valid]))
    # A little rounding can make an analytic unit circulation exceed one by epsilon.
    circulation[valid] = np.clip(circulation[valid], 0, 1)
    centroid_speed = np.linalg.norm(centroid_v, axis=1)/length_scale
    centroid_speed[~all_velocity] = np.nan
    first = ordered.groupby("frame", sort=True)[["time", "segment"]].first()
    polarization = np.hypot(np.cos(a["ANGLE"]).mean(axis=1), np.sin(a["ANGLE"]).mean(axis=1))
    if "heading_valid" in ordered:
        polarization[~ordered.heading_valid.to_numpy(dtype=bool).reshape(len(frames), len(fish)).all(axis=1)] = np.nan
    return pd.DataFrame({
        "frame": frames, "time": first.time.to_numpy(), "segment": first.segment.to_numpy(),
        "polarization": polarization,
        "spread_bl": np.sqrt((radius**2).mean(axis=1))/length_scale,
        "centroid_speed_bl_s": centroid_speed, "circulation": circulation,
        "eligible_fraction": fractions, "signed_circulation": signed,
        "iid_direction_reference": reference,
    })


def spatial(tracks, length_scale, extent=10, bins=40):
    """All-neighbor occupancy in focal forward/right coordinates.

    histogram[forward_bin, right_bin] contains raw ordered-pair counts. density
    divides by all focal observations and bin area in square body lengths.
    """
    _scale(length_scale)
    if not np.isfinite(extent) or extent <= 0 or not isinstance(bins, (int, np.integer)) or bins < 1:
        raise ValueError("Positive extent and integer bin count required")
    frames, fish, _, a = _dense(tracks, ["X", "Y", "ANGLE"])
    if not all(np.isfinite(values).all() for values in a.values()):
        raise ValueError("Nonfinite spatial inputs")
    edges = np.linspace(-extent, extent, bins+1)
    counts = np.zeros((bins, bins), dtype=float)
    nearest = np.full((len(frames), len(fish)), np.nan)
    off_diagonal = ~np.eye(len(fish), dtype=bool)
    # Chunk frame dimension so larger schools do not allocate a trial-sized NxN tensor.
    for start in range(0, len(frames), 128):
        stop = min(start+128, len(frames))
        x, y, theta = (a[column][start:stop] for column in ["X", "Y", "ANGLE"])
        dx = (x[:, None, :] - x[:, :, None])/length_scale
        dy = (y[:, None, :] - y[:, :, None])/length_scale
        distance = np.hypot(dx, dy)
        distance[:, np.arange(len(fish)), np.arange(len(fish))] = np.inf
        if len(fish) > 1:
            nearest[start:stop] = distance.min(axis=2)
        cosine, sine = np.cos(theta)[:, :, None], np.sin(theta)[:, :, None]
        forward = (dx*cosine + dy*sine)[:, off_diagonal].ravel()
        right = (-dx*sine + dy*cosine)[:, off_diagonal].ravel()
        counts += np.histogram2d(forward, right, bins=(edges, edges))[0]
    focal_count = len(frames)*len(fish)
    pairs = focal_count*(len(fish)-1)
    bin_area = (edges[1]-edges[0])**2
    return {
        "nearest": pd.DataFrame({"frame": np.repeat(frames, len(fish)), "id": np.tile(fish, len(frames)),
                                 "distance_bl": nearest.ravel()}),
        "histogram": counts, "density": counts/(focal_count*bin_area),
        "bin_edges": edges, "focal_count": focal_count,
        "shown_pair_fraction": float(counts.sum()/pairs) if pairs else float("nan"),
    }


def visibility(tracks, network):
    """Observer out-degree including zeros, restricted to covered frames."""
    _single_trial(tracks)
    if network.duplicated(["frame", "id1", "id2"]).any():
        raise ValueError("Duplicate visibility edges")
    if (network.id1 == network.id2).any():
        raise ValueError("Self visibility edges")
    track_keys = pd.MultiIndex.from_frame(tracks[["frame", "id"]])
    for column in ["id1", "id2"]:
        if not pd.MultiIndex.from_frame(network[["frame", column]]).isin(track_keys).all():
            raise ValueError("Dangling visibility endpoint")
    covered = tracks[tracks.frame.isin(network.frame.unique())].sort_values(["frame", "id"])
    keys = pd.MultiIndex.from_frame(covered[["frame", "id"]])
    counts = network.groupby(["frame", "id1"]).size().reindex(keys, fill_value=0)
    degree = covered[["frame", "id"]].reset_index(drop=True).copy()
    degree["degree"] = counts.to_numpy(dtype=int)
    per_frame = degree.groupby("frame", as_index=False).agg(mean_degree=("degree", "mean"))
    pairs = pd.MultiIndex.from_frame(network[["frame", "id1", "id2"]])
    reverse = pd.MultiIndex.from_arrays([network.frame, network.id2, network.id1])
    reciprocity = float(reverse.isin(pairs).mean()) if len(network) else float("nan")
    return {"degree": degree, "per_frame": per_frame, "reciprocal_edge_fraction": reciprocity}


def heading_diagnostics(k, length_scale, low_speed=.5):
    """Describe jumps and sustained travel opposite heading, without exclusion.

    Reverse motion can be real backward movement, a tracking error, or another
    mismatch. These diagnostics do not identify proven head-tail flips. The
    velocity eligibility denominator is computed before any heading jump mask.
    """
    _single_trial(k)
    _scale(length_scale)
    if not np.isfinite(low_speed) or low_speed <= 0:
        raise ValueError("Low-speed threshold must be finite and positive")
    angle = k.ANGLE.to_numpy(dtype=float)
    vx, vy = k.vx.to_numpy(dtype=float), k.vy.to_numpy(dtype=float)
    if not np.isfinite(angle).all():
        raise ValueError("Nonfinite heading diagnostic inputs")
    speed = np.hypot(vx, vy)/length_scale
    eligible = np.isfinite(speed) & (speed >= low_speed)
    slip = (np.arctan2(vy, vx) - angle + np.pi) % (2*np.pi) - np.pi
    reverse = eligible & (np.abs(slip) > np.pi/2)
    unmasked = k.heading_valid.to_numpy(dtype=bool) if "heading_valid" in k else np.ones(len(k), dtype=bool)
    frames = k.frame.to_numpy()
    events = []
    best = {int(fish): {"id": int(fish), "start": None, "end": None, "segment": None, "n": 0}
            for fish in np.sort(k.id.unique())}
    for (fish, segment), positions in k.groupby(["id", "segment"], sort=True).indices.items():
        positions = positions[np.argsort(frames[positions], kind="stable")]
        block_frames = frames[positions]
        step = (np.diff(angle[positions]) + np.pi) % (2*np.pi) - np.pi
        jumps = np.flatnonzero((np.diff(block_frames) == 1) & (np.abs(step) > np.pi/2))
        for index in jumps:
            events.append({"id": int(fish), "segment": int(segment),
                           "prev_frame": int(block_frames[index]), "frame": int(block_frames[index+1]),
                           "signed_wrapped_step": float(step[index])})
        # Retain only reverse samples. A false/ineligible sample or frame gap
        # breaks a run even if reverse motion resumes on the next stored row.
        indices = np.flatnonzero(reverse[positions])
        if not len(indices):
            continue
        cuts = np.flatnonzero((np.diff(indices) != 1) | (np.diff(block_frames[indices]) != 1))+1
        for run in np.split(indices, cuts):
            record = {"id": int(fish), "start": int(block_frames[run[0]]),
                      "end": int(block_frames[run[-1]]), "segment": int(segment), "n": int(len(run))}
            old = best[int(fish)]
            if record["n"] > old["n"] or (record["n"] == old["n"] and record["start"] < old["start"]):
                best[int(fish)] = record
    denominator = int(eligible.sum())
    return {
        "low_speed_bl_s": float(low_speed), "heading_events": events,
        "speed_eligible_rows_before_heading_mask": denominator,
        "reverse_motion_rows": int(reverse.sum()),
        "reverse_motion_fraction": float(reverse.sum()/denominator) if denominator else None,
        "reverse_motion_rows_outside_jump_mask": int((reverse & unmasked).sum()),
        "longest_reverse_runs_per_fish": list(best.values()),
        "interpretation": "Flags reverse travel relative to tracked heading, not proven head-tail flips. "
                          "Sustained intervals are not automatically excluded without video validation.",
        "mask_policy": "Finite speed >= threshold; heading_valid is not used in the primary denominator or runs.",
    }

===== END SOURCE =====

# Focused confirmation review: comparison-results narrative fixes (B1–B4)

## Verdict: **PASS**

The corrected descriptive report can be shared locally with its stated caveats. B1–B4 are each addressed. Every number I checked in the corrected README agrees with the supplied tables, `validation.json` and code. I found no remaining material wording or numerical contradiction. The optional qualifications add no unsupported biological claim. One of them names a biological possibility in hedged form, which I discuss under N2.

The three notes at the end are non-blocking.

**Basis:** everything below is my own static reading of the supplied text, tables, JSON and source code. I ran nothing and viewed no images. Test counts, hash checks, pilot parity, the 50 sampled-P recomputations and the coordinator's plot inspection are **coordinator-reported execution evidence**, not mine.

---

## B1 — P smoothing presented correctly ✓

**What the README says now**
- §1 lists only these robustness checks: raw-heading and affected-fish-only summaries, invalid-length frame exclusion, and the 17,999-frame prefix.
- It then states: "P uses raw headings: changing the smoothing window changes P only through the heading-jump exclusion mask, so the window sweep is not an independent test of smoothing the alignment signal."
- The Monday summary no longer mentions smoothing.

**Code check (my reasoning)**
- In `metrics.collective`, `polarization = hypot(mean cos ANGLE, mean sin ANGLE)` uses raw `ANGLE`. Only the `heading_valid` mask is applied afterwards.
- In `kinematics`, `heading_jump` is computed from raw `ANGLE` steps, so it does not depend on the window. `window` enters only through `np.convolve(..., np.ones(window))`, which sets the mask width.
- The README sentence therefore describes the code accurately.

**On the coordinator declining "cannot fail by construction"**
I agree with the coordinator. A wider mask excludes more frames, which could in principle shift the median. "Persistence is expected but not guaranteed" is the more precise framing, and the README's wording ("not an independent test") is sufficient.

## B2 — The N=151 result is in the short summary ✓

The Monday summary now reads: "The single 151-fish recording's alignment fell within the 70-fish range, so it does not extend the ordering; it also has a different camera."

- 151 median P = 0.6406, which lies inside the 70-fish range 0.509–0.694 ✓.
- The new lead paragraph also says circulation lies within the 70-fish range: C = 0.4837, inside 0.379–0.781 ✓.

## B3 — Precision ✓

**P coverage column.** Recomputed from `polarization_eligible_frame_fraction`:

|---|---:|---:|
| 0066 | 0.99902 | 99.90% ✓ |
| 0105 | 0.99805 | 99.80% ✓ |

| 0084 | 0.99130 | 99.13% ✓ |
| 0115 | 0.99797 | 99.80% ✓ |
| 0120 | 0.99642 | 99.64% ✓ |
| 0103 | 0.99032 | 99.03% ✓ |
| 0107 | 0.99292 | 99.29% ✓ |
| 0124 | 0.99197 | 99.20% ✓ |
| 151 | 0.98044 | 98.04% ✓ |

- §5's "98.0–99.95%" agrees with the table.

- I accept the coordinator's point that the earlier "≤2.23e-16" was a valid upper bound, not an error.

## B4 — Spread lead sentence ✓

§3 now leads with: "school-spread medians separate between N=10 and 30, but the N=30 and 70 ranges overlap at full duration and separate only in the matched prefix." It then adds that some growth is geometrically expected at comparable spacing.

- 10 → 30: 2.202–2.970 vs 3.707–5.069, nonoverlapping ✓.
